data-words=\"6810\">recognised</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-529\" data-words=\"529\">as</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-2900\" data-words=\"2900\">essential to</a> <a class=\"vocab\" navigation\"><button data-go=\"196\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"198\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x035c01","title":"1. A Goat and an Unreliable Bridge","volume":"婚后外传：山路上的山羊剧团","anchor":"chapter-0199","paragraphs":["The troupe set out for the hill village with a broken crown, six costumes and a goat-shaped puppet. Cassian had expected a quiet weekend. \"On the contrary,\" Mara warned, \"I have plans.\" Mara had told him the local folk choir needed someone to carry scenery, which was true in a strategically incomplete way. He discovered the rest when the troupe director asked him to rehearse the landlord's song.","\"I married an incurable menace,\" he told her.","\"Faithful, though. Very important in a union.\"","The minibus climbed above the river through rich country vegetation. Sheep of a local breed grazed by the gate. Pia, a zoology graduate who was currently unemployed, pointed out a cluster of nests near the old bridge. A troop of village children waited to join the walk. They had invited the performers for a field weekend organised by a small institute. Botany and zoology would precede the feast; the play afterward concerned a landlord with more appetite than wisdom.","Dana, their driver, had once worked for the Transportation Security Administration in the United States. She now ran a tiny transport outfit and knew half the townsfolk. She called the group her theatrical entourage, though its only celebrity was a puppet. Her former job did not make her an expert on every danger. It did make her unamused by a costume sword protruding into the aisle. She moved it before starting the engine. \"An avant-garde injury would still be an injury,\" she said.","June had come to write about the weekend. Her newsroom wanted a short story, but the troupe's repertoire tempted her into a longer one. Its members called themselves a tribe of terrible singers; they were in fact competent performers who enjoyed insulting themselves. Their organisation had invited a mixed clientele, including retired workers, students and people too timid to enter a theatre alone. June doubted her readership would call this a quiet retreat. The public forum was free. June would define the trip as a holiday for people who enjoyed carrying things. The longer programme in a magazine remained paywalled, which annoyed June's grandmother.","Cassian browsed the printed guide while Mara tied a knot in the goat's loose ear. The story used feudalism as its setting and capitalism as the subject of the second act. It explored unionism through a quarrel over harvest hours. A union organiser had helped with the dialogue about labour. An uncompetitive ticket price helped country visitors come. The student senate had contributed travel money, but no financial institution had funded the supposedly lavish stage. The sponsor had offered to engrave names on a grand plaque, whereas the troupe wanted cushions. It was going to be a barn and a heap of cushions. The fees kept the journey affordable for needy guests without asking performers to work for nothing.","\"I shall redefine nobility,\" Cassian announced, studying his landlord's lines.","\"Try first to remember where you stand,\" Mara said.","The sky was saturated with pale grey cloud. A sober farmer at the village gate warned them of a blocked upper road. A dealership van had reported loose stones after last night's rain. The driver of another bus waved them on, saying the danger was exaggerated. Dana stayed where she was. Widespread familiarity with a route did not stop a hillside changing. The prevalent local habit was to trust the farmer who had just walked it.","The group would continue on foot by the lower path. \"This is not to say every visitor should turn a holiday into an expedition,\" Dana added. Dana's minibus would return to the settlement for anyone who preferred it. Their walk would be limited to the signed route, not the unexplored ravine visible beyond it. Pia knew the flora along that route intimately. She had grown up here and could identify where the stream normally crossed it.","She had made a fruitless attempt to find the spare hat in a suitcase. Mara changed out of her superhigh heels. Cassian watched with open disappointment. \"There goes half my hope of carrying you.\"","\"You have the goat.\"","A retired army cook offered to carry the hamper. A navy contingent had already arranged a small fleet of river boats for the evening visitors below. Rook, who had volunteered one trip, would wait at the landing. A police patrol checked the lower turning, while a troop of dancers stretched by the bus. The children walked in a loose formation behind their teacher. The village population nearly doubled on festival days. The progression seemed simple: walk, lunch, singing and then the play. June wrote it down, thereby almost guaranteeing that events would disagree.","A sick child needed to return with Dana. The boy was not abandoned as an inconvenience; his aunt went with him, and the teacher adjusted the groups. The remaining workforce of volunteers was small but willing. Pia asked them to collaborate by keeping children within sight rather than appointing six rival leaders. Oftentimes, being unemployed caused mental strain quite separate from a person’s ability. Pia was trying not to worry about that today. A bunch of dancers agreed, then argued about which of them was most likely to get lost.","At the first stream, they watched a variety of fish species beneath the clear water. An old guild bridge crossed to the other side. Its timber bracket looked sound from the path, but Pia walked ahead to inspect the landing. She did not allege a fault; she saw one. A fresh lump of earth had fallen away from the far support. The structure had shifted.","The choir stopped singing. Mara looked beyond the bridge and saw a pair of children already on the opposite bank. Their teacher had taken them across before the rest arrived. A branch cracked above them, followed by the low sound of moving stone. The puppet goat slid from Cassian's shoulder as he turned.","\"Don't come back across,\" Pia called.","Her voice was no longer timid. She needed the townsfolk to hear exactly what she was saying. The troupe listened. Dana's minibus was out of sight, and the bridge had begun to move."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1017,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0199\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：山路上的山羊剧团</div><h1>1. The Longer Way Home","volume":"婚后外传：山路上的山羊剧团","anchor":"chapter-0200","paragraphs":["The teacher drew the children away from the bank as the far end of the bridge sagged. A stone struck the water below. Cassian caught Mara's wrist before she stepped onto the first plank, and she stopped herself in the same instant. The instinct to reach someone was not a reason to follow them onto a failing structure.","Pia pointed upstream. A track led from the far bank to a shepherd's hut. She knew it from botany walks with her grandfather. \"This isn’t a lost world. On the contrary, people work here,\" Pia said. It was not an unexplored wilderness, whatever the brochure called the valley. The troop could shelter there while the troupe returned to the village. The teacher could take the children toward shelter while the group on this side contacted the village. The route would be longer, but it avoided the steep bank.","A timid boy did not want to leave the stream without the puppet goat, which he had been promised for the play. Cassian lifted its ridiculous head. \"It is a faithful animal,\" he called. \"It will wait.\"","Mara looked at him with an affection so sudden he noticed even over the water. He could make a child smile without pretending the danger was funny. She kissed his shoulder as they turned back.","The country air smelled of wet earth. They could elect to wait indoors now, not follow another path. They needed to redefine the afternoon. June's phone found a weak signal by a bare rock, enough for a call to Dana. The former Transportation Security Administration officer knew the lower approach and would alert the local rescue team. Her outfit could carry those returning to the settlement; the stranded teacher would follow the hut path. A plan did not require an army of volunteers to run in six directions.","The director kept the troupe together. The union organiser helped divide the carried equipment, taking a heap of costumes from an elderly singer. In addition to the scenery, they had the hamper and a small first-aid bag. The navy visitors at the river would send word to Rook. Her fleet of one useful boat could bring the returning group home later, if the water and weather allowed. The decision was contingent on what she found, not on how much the audience wanted a story.","A wide band of rain moved toward them. The vegetation grew dense beside the path, and everyone's sleeves were soon saturated. The local senate had funded the school’s extra radio last year. The teacher's bright coat disappeared around a bend on the far bank. Mara felt a sick pull of fear, then watched Pia name the next marker calmly. Her knowledge of the ecosystem was practical, not decorative. The fish species in this stream mattered because they had led her family along it for years.","\"I used to think I'd wasted my zoology course,\" Pia said as they walked. \"No permanent work. Too many applications.\"","\"You're unemployed,\" June replied. \"That isn't the same as unskilled.\"","The comment made Pia cry for approximately three seconds. Then she laughed and wiped her nose with an aggressively unromantic noise. The townsfolk had known her as a quiet child. She would have to redefine that picture for them herself.","At the ridge, a lump of mud took Cassian's foot sideways. He recovered without falling, but the goat rolled downhill. Mara caught its cord around a low branch and hauled it back. The avant-garde creature now wore a crown of leaves. A choir member said it would enhance the play. Cassian tied a better knot and refused to surrender its honour to the weather.","The radio message from the rescue team was brief. The teacher and children had reached the hut. Trained personnel would meet them from the upper farm track; nobody needed to recross the bridge. Nobody asked a superhigh platform to become safe by wishing. A sober relief moved through the party. The preceding half-hour had felt significantly longer. Widespread alarm at the village could now become useful preparation instead of rumour.","Dana met them at the old guild workshop. The building had a dry room, a furnace and a cupboard of cups. A circus poster hung over its cracked bracket. Its clientele usually consisted of carpenters, not a wet theatre tribe, but the owner opened the door. The student senate’s earlier donation had paid for extra blankets. He was not rich. He did have a bench and tea, and he could sympathize with needing both. The choir's variety of shivering voices supplied its own entertainment.","The local history display described feudalism through old rent documents and capitalism through a later factory. Cassian read a solemn caption while water dripped from his noble sleeve. Mara said this was the nobility's most faithful public appearance. He promised retaliation after she dried his coat. She stood close enough to help, then slipped both cold hands beneath his waistcoat. His complaint became a laugh against her hair.","The organisation cancelled the afternoon forum. Its paywalled long guide was now less useful than the hand-written notice on the door. \"This is not to say the work inside it has lost value,\" June told the director. Worry could recur after the immediate danger passed, whereas tea could at least warm a hand. The prevalent assumption that a publication could solve every immediate question was simply misplaced. The paper notice told parents where the children were. Oftentimes, the shortest message carried the most weight.","A dealership mechanic brought a spare heater. A financial institution offered its empty meeting room as overflow space, and a police officer checked the roadside closure. The commonality was simple: everyone wanted the children warm. No bureaucracy was conjured into a villain; people who knew their jobs did them. A cluster of townsfolk came to help, adding dry coats to the workforce. The troupe would collaborate with whoever needed carrying or feeding. Its repertoire could wait.","Outside, a church bell rang twice. Pia looked up from the zoology guide she had used to entertain the children. The second message had arrived: the teacher and both pupils were safe. The village would have its feast after all, though nobody had imagined this route to it."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1039,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0200\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：山路上的山羊剧团</div><h1>2. The Goat Takes a Bow","volume":"婚后外传：山路上的山羊剧团","anchor":"chapter-0201","paragraphs":["By evening, the goat puppet was the most celebrated performer in the village. One of the rescued children had decorated it with a bunch of paper flowers. Cassian was asked to carry it into the hall as a heroic entrance. Mara accompanied him with the solemn face of a woman trying very hard not to laugh. The townsfolk applauded. He bowed, and the goat's head fell over one eye.","\"An exquisite portrait of nobility,\" she whispered.","\"I expect this to remain in your mental archive forever.\"","\"Intimately filed.\"","The troupe had elected to perform a shortened play. Nobody would be punished for wanting to rest. The preceding hour had brought a quiet progression from fright to appetite. The teacher and the children watched from the front row, wrapped in dry blankets, with their families beside them. A circus performer borrowed from the neighbouring camp taught the youngest guest how to balance a paper crown. The skill was substantially safer than the boy's proposed career as a bridge rescuer.","Pia sat near the door, still timid when someone thanked her. The zoology graduate had not saved everyone single-handedly. She had named a safe direction, and the teacher, driver and rescue team had each done the next part. The local population was small enough to know exactly who had carried what. She could accept her own share without shrinking it or claiming theirs. The unemployed guide had begun the day wondering whether any of her knowledge mattered. That question felt less plausible now.","The fleet had stayed tied up because the river was running high. Rook came to the feast by road instead, an old navy cap in her pocket. She was part of the small entourage returning from the river. A retired army cook handed her a plate before she could volunteer for another task. Their commonality was not an appetite for danger. It was an appreciation of coming back with everyone they had started with. The union organiser raised a cup to that, and the village answered.","Dana sat with a troop of children who wanted to hear about her former work at the Transportation Security Administration. She gave them a short, ordinary answer about checking bags and following instructions, then asked what they had seen on the walk. She was not going to turn an American agency into the explanation for everything she could do. They told her about the fish instead. It was a better conversation.","June sent a short update to the newsroom. Its readership would hear about the bridge closure before receiving the longer account. The story was not paywalled. The magazine's separate illustrated essay would remain so, supporting the work that produced it. \"This is not to say everyone needs both,\" June told her editor. People waiting for news of a child needed the first thing first. The distinction seemed obvious now and had been less clear in a meeting last week.","At the meal, a local institute offered Pia a paid series of school walks after the route reopened and inspections were complete. The offer would be limited to the days funded. She would browse the terms tomorrow, with a friend if she wanted one. The superhigh excitement of being asked did not have to silence practical questions. Her present need was supper, not a ceremonial signature over soup.","The folk choir began softly, standing in a curved formation. The scene about unionism belonged to actors who had negotiated their own pay. Their repertoire included a song about an old sailor who lost every argument to a goat. Cassian alleged that Rowan had selected it. Rowan denied involvement with no apparent conviction. Mara would not sympathize. She sat on her husband's knee for the final verse and observed that his union with her had been a fine artistic decision. His hand tightened warmly at her waist.","The little environmental forum became a conversation around the meal. A fisherman described the flora along the quieter banks, and Pia explained how the population of one familiar bird changed with the seasons. A nursery owner spoke about the breed of sheep that browsed his orchard. No one called the valley empty because a visitor had not explored it. Its unexplored corners were unexplored by them, not necessarily by the people who worked there.","The performers would redefine their next visit around a shorter, easier route. The trust that came from this day was sober rather than triumphant. Saturated ground and loose stone were widespread after the rain; the prevalent desire to continue a promised outing had almost concealed that. Bureaucracy had not caused this; a damaged bridge had. A new sign would help, but the staff would also ask someone who had walked the path recently. The ecosystem would survive without every visitor taking the shortest path. Superhigh confidence did not improve a muddy road.","They could define the event as a journey repaired by many hands. A school guild offered to engrave a small thank-you plate for the rescue team. The teacher proposed the children's names on a different class project about the walk. Their fright did not have to be recorded as foolishness. Their uncompetitive entry fee would stay low for needy families. A small clay tribe of imaginary animals would do: the goat, a bird and an indignant fish. They could originate a story in which the smallest creature had the most sensible advice.","Mara and Cassian left after the last song. His borrowed outfit smelled faintly of rain and theatre paint. She was carrying the broken crown, which the children had insisted belonged to her. Mara was tempted to keep the goat, but this was unlikely to please its owner. They helped set up the empty costume box and cross-check the passengers. The minibus would take them home; no long, fruitless search for a perfect ending was required. The same alarm need not recur tomorrow. In addition to gratitude, Pia felt significantly hungry. They had tea, a seat together and tired bodies willing to rest.","He leaned toward her as the vehicle started. \"Next weekend, no scenery.\"","\"On the contrary,\" she said. \"I have a delightful idea involving a bed and a view.\"","He considered it, then kissed her until Dana coughed pointedly from the front. The troupe cheered from the back, and Mara laughed against his mouth."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1055,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0201\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：山路上的山羊剧团</div><h1>3. A Ransom Paid in Tea","volume":"婚后外传：失踪公爵与木头珍珠","anchor":"chapter-0202","paragraphs":["The letter informed Mara that her husband had been kidnapped and would be exchanged only for a pearl, a kiss and a decent cup of tea. Cassian was standing beside her when she opened it. He read over her shoulder, considered the terms and said the tea would be the difficult part.","The theatre club had arranged a mystery evening at Ben's toy museum. Ben, a soccer-mad Englander with a magnificent white beard, had promised a high-quality adventure. The game was limited to the public rooms. Cassian had agreed to play the missing duke. Mara was to find him somewhere in the multi-storey house. \"If you escape first, I shall eat my words,\" she told him.","\"Which ones?\"","\"Any of the generous things I said at breakfast.\"","Rowan tried to disorganize their entrance by announcing the wrong duke. Ben led them past a model supersonic aircraft to the costume room. He had founded the toy company, then discovered that boss-board relations required skills quite different from carving a horse. Retirement had improved both his temper and his marriage. The archive file headed IN RE THE BLUE CABINET concerned an old ownership dispute; today's game borrowed its theatrical language, not its contents. Nobody was holding a real legal hearing beside a rocking horse.","The event would run from mid to late afternoon, ending with a banquet. In general, the performers preferred games to speeches. Its proceeds would consolidate a small scholarship fund. Ben liked games more than do-gooding speeches. The lighting would enhance the final garden scene, Ben promised. He had chosen to devote his birthday to something people could enjoy. Ethically, that was easier to defend than making guests applaud his generosity for forty minutes. Rowan had already been warned not to fabricate an introduction about \"a lifetime of selfless achievement.\"","In the cramped wardrobe, Mara found a coat with a peculiar hidden seam. Intuitively, she checked whether it concealed a game token. It did. The staff had supplied a ridiculous cape to exemplify the fictional duke’s vanity. Cassian looked offended. \"I haven't been kidnapped yet.\"","\"Foresighted reconnaissance.\"","\"Merciless woman.\"","She kissed him. His disappointment became less convincing.","A publisher had donated the old clue cards, and a university drama society supplied actors. The coalition included a student from a pharma company's training programme, who cheerfully played a suspicious physician without giving real medical advice. An orchestra recording came from a rights-cleared disc. The museum bureau's printed guide stated which doors were scenery and which were working exits. Common-sense precautions made the house a playground instead of a hazard.","The organisers did carry insurance. Ben did not intend that fact to replace checking a loose stair rail. He asked a carpenter to fix it before the audience arrived. The paper roses were vulnerable to moisture. The humid morning had swollen one wooden door, and a staff member marked it out of the route. Conversely, another door could stand open without disorganizing the game. The decision-making was not glamorous, but it let the attractive parts happen.","Cassian was led away by two actors in theatrical military coats. Mara waved. A primal delight in pursuit rose through her, surprising and welcome. Her mission was ridiculous, and the man she wanted at its end was hers. Indeed, the whole afternoon felt like an excuse to cherish him in public while pretending she intended to defeat him.","She opened her first bundle of clues. One card told her to codify the strange symbols into an alphabet. Another warned that a clock might falsely signify urgency. A third asked her to redefine a prisoner as a guest. She could go about solving the first by comparing repeated marks. She did not need an instantaneous revelation to look clever. She needed a pencil.","Rowan offered an intriguing answer involving a family lineage, an enemy and a secret regime. Mara declined. The museum's convention was that the clue giver never lied about a marked object. She would figure out the mechanism before inventing a dynasty. A faded array of painted birds formed the key; their colours corresponded to the dots on her card. Likewise, the toy foxes identified the next room.","The locked cabinet contained an old letter about a National Trust visit. Its purpose in the game was to reveal a date, not give anyone authority over the museum. Ben had refused to cut cost by borrowing a photograph without permission. High-quality materials could be ethically sourced even for a silly afternoon. The note had been licensed for this use. Her better-behaved ally at the card table held the page flat. Mara cherished the tiny handwritten correction in the margin more than its official heading.","She reached a landing where a mob of delighted guests blocked the view. Ben asked them to scale back on enthusiastic crowding and make room for someone else to look. The room beyond was a miniature meritocracy in which nobody cared about Mara's nationality or title. The rule could take account of a visitor’s pace without giving away the answer. They cared whether she could solve the puzzle. The hierarchy began and ended with a child who held the only pencil sharpener.","On the matter of the missing duke, a butler would allude to nothing except tea. Mara tried generously offering him a biscuit. He accepted the biscuit and remained loyal to his script. She deplored his professional integrity. Her frustrated sigh made him grin.","The next corridor was thorny with paper roses. A notice said that a forced entry would impinge on the actor's safety. She used the visible latch instead. It seemed unfeasible that Cassian could have reached the final room already. Then she heard him laughing behind the wall.","\"You are going to grow up one day,\" she called.","\"Willfully unlikely,\" he answered.","The clock chimed.
